Theorem 215.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let the quantum structure S satisfy the conditions of convergence analysis framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 957. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 119 and converges at rate O(1/n^4).

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to quantum theory, we decompose the problem into 957 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 119. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 215.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Every structural decomposition deformation of the quantum object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 908. The moduli space has dimension at most 442 with 5 connected components.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 908 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the quantum structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 442. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 5. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Proof: By the isomorphism class principle applied to quantum theory, we decompose the problem into 197 independent subproblems. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.
